# Abstract

This gazetteer compiles population estimates for 1.359 excavated
settlements across the 207,6k km² river basins of Polotsk, Turov and
Belarusian Rus from the Final Palaeolithic (10000BC) to the early Grand
Duchy of Lithuania (1400AD)
[\[1\]](#ref-shtykhov1992goroda)[\[2\]](#ref-zagorulsky2001arkheologiya)[\[3\]](#ref-lysenko2004drevniy).
The prehistoric and Iron Age series cover 24 representative sites,
including the mammoth- and reindeer-hunter camps of Yurevichi and
Berdizh (40 - 65 inhabitants in 10000BC), the waterlogged Neolithic pile
dwellings of the Krivina peat basin at Asavets (160 - 340 inhabitants in
2500BC), the Milograd, Zarubintsy and Brushed Pottery hillforts (320 -
560 inhabitants in 500BC - 200AD) and the tenth-century craft complex on
the Menka river (2,4k in 1000AD)
[\[4\]](#ref-charniauski1997arkhealogiya)[\[5\]](#ref-isaenko1999arkhealogiya)[\[6\]](#ref-zagorulsky1982vozniknovenie).

Across the medieval fortified towns (*gorodishcha*) and rural
settlements occupied between 800AD and 1400AD, urban populations reached
their pre-Mongol peak in 1200AD under the Izyaslavichi princes of
Polotsk and the Rurikid rulers of Turov-Pinsk and Black Ruthenia, led by
Polotsk (25k across 190 ha), Turov (12k), Vitebsk (9,5k), Minsk (9k),
Grodno (8,5k), Pinsk (8k), Berestye (7,5k), Novogrudok (7,5k) and Gomel
(6,5k)
[\[7\]](#ref-tarasov1998polotsk)[\[8\]](#ref-lysenko1985berestye)[\[9\]](#ref-gurevich1981drevniy).
While Batu Khan’s columns of 1239-1241AD sacked Gomel (falling to 3,5k
in 1250AD), Turov (7k), Mozyr (2,1k) and Berestye (5,2k) along the
southern frontier, the northern and western capitals of Polotsk (22k in
1250AD, 23k in 1400AD), Vitebsk (9,5k in 1400AD), Minsk (8,8k), Grodno
(8,5k) and Novogrudok (rising to 9k in 1300AD as Mindaugas’s early seat)
escaped destruction and anchored the early Lithuanian-Ruthenian realm.

# Methodology

Across the 207,6k km² river basins of Polotsk, Turov and Belarusian Rus,
archaeological excavations and chronicle records document 1.359
principal settlements between the Final Palaeolithic (10000BC), the
Mongol campaigns of 1238-1241 and the late thirteenth-century
principalities (1240AD - 1400AD)
[\[1\]](#ref-shtykhov1992goroda)[\[2\]](#ref-zagorulsky2001arkheologiya)[\[3\]](#ref-lysenko2004drevniy).

# Results

## Summary by Chronological Horizon

| Chronological Horizon | Attested Sites |
|:---|:---|
| I. Palaeolithic, Neolithic, Trypillia and Bronze Age (10000BC - 1000BC) | 187 |
| II\. Early Iron Age, Scythian, Roman-Era and Antiquity (999BC - 400AD) | 159 |
| III\. Early Slavonic and Pre-Mongol Kievan Rus (401AD - 1240AD) | 855 |
| IV\. Post-Mongol Appanage and Early Lithuanian Transition (1241AD - 1400AD) | 158 |
| Total Gazetteer Settlements | 1.359 |

Chronological distribution of the 1.359 catalogued settlements and
archaeological habitation sites in Polotsk, Turov and Belarusian Rus by
horizon of earliest documented stratum (10000BC - 1400AD).
